## Actual ID collection and tenancy retention — 22 September 2026

- **User decision / implementation:** Brett rejected the sample-only experience: he is making a real booking and wants to upload his actual ID. He explicitly selected retention **throughout the tenancy**. Do not describe him as a tester or ask again for approval of this requirement. Added TENANCY retention with nullable expiry and a database consistency constraint. Copies survive actual handover and ACTIVE/NOTICE_GIVEN tenancies, then erase on recorded closure/cancellation. Fixed-period copies retain their acknowledged lifetime. Staff can privately preview retained copies after handover with existing permissions/audit; onboarding review cannot change them after conversion. Cancelled/expired/abandoned bookings retain erasure protections. The deployed notice is `docs/IDENTITY_DOCUMENT_CUSTOMER_NOTICE.md`, using confirmed operating company/contact. This records the business instruction and does not invent separate legal sign-off on wording or immediate backup erasure.

- **Scope and decisions:** Brett requested completion of the move-in/physical-access backlog item. - **Implementation:** isolated branch `codex/facial-access-completion-20260922`, based on canonical `blendproperty/stor24-portal` main `1227f1074fa7c260f470618e87e77756e9b46300`. Adds an authenticated, booking-bound My STOR24 photo queue; explicit policy-hash consent; decoded/re-encoded JPEG/PNG with byte/pixel caps and metadata removal; booking/version-bound AES-GCM storage; facility/permission-scoped audited previews; exact-version staff review; atomic handover-to-pending-activation binding; post-handover photo review; replacement invalidation; withdrawal, rejection and expiry erasure. New collection requires approved organisation policy configuration, encryption and a fresh deletion-worker heartbeat. No policy or retention period is assumed approved. A separate credential-protected worker and five-minute VPS timer installer are supplied. Staff and customer screens use real 400/500/700 brand fonts, with updated read-only guidance.
- **Provider boundary:** disables the old direct staff-upload enrolment service/API. Existing code could retry partially completed person/face/permission creation without durable recovery state and used staff-attested consent. The new queue has no enabled provider consumer and never marks access active or sends access-ready notifications. Multiple recorded grants sharing a provider person block legacy revocation for reconciliation. HikCentral transport checks the exact certificate pin before sending any HTTP request, refuses redirects, caps response size/time and requires explicit provider success. This is transport hardening, not completion of the provider lifecycle. Durable staged activation, suspension/restoration, revocation, erasure, device distribution/readback and uncertain-outcome reconciliation remain to be implemented against the confirmed installed API contract.
- **Testing:** full local `npm run check` passed typecheck, lint (zero errors, six pre-existing warnings), 341 application tests and production build. Fresh isolated PostgreSQL migration bootstrap passed, followed by all 25 photo/handover tests, covering customer/organisation/facility isolation, real-payment eligibility, future move-in, concurrent replacement, stale approval, duplicate handover, post-handover approval, withdrawal and live-database ciphertext erasure. Actual-component browser checks passed at 1440/390px: real fonts, no horizontal overflow, legal hold, private preview, stale-review rejection, cleared preview, read-only controls and unticked consent. Desktop/mobile screenshots were visually inspected. Updated guided-help browser suite passed with zero operational writes and zero browser errors. Worker shell syntax passed. ## Netcash Pay Now sandbox proof — 7 September 2026

- **Scope:** this closes a Pay Now-specific sandbox-proof slice only. It does not change Netcash's BLOCKED status in `docs/STOR24_OUTSTANDING_TASKS.md` item 1 or the Integrations screen's "Configuration required" state — per the governance boundary already recorded in `claude/invoicing-statements-scope.md` §0, moving those requires business sign-off in addition to sandbox proof, and this is a single-product, single-successful-transaction proof, not full failure-case, reconciliation or exception-ownership coverage.
- **Contract correction (implementation):** `src/lib/payments/netcash-client.ts`'s Pay Now functions were checked directly against Netcash's published Pay Now eCommerce and TransactionStatus/Check documentation and rewritten to match it: Pay Now is a **browser form POST** to `https://paynow.netcash.co.za/site/paynow.aspx` (fields `m1`/`m2`/`p2`/`p3`/`p4`/`Budget`/`m9`), not the server-to-server REST call originally scaffolded; `ReturnUrl`/`CancelUrl`/`NotifyUrl` are not per-request parameters, only a one-time NetConnector Profile configuration in the Netcash portal; `p2`/`p3` length limits (25/50 chars) are now enforced rather than silently truncated. Netcash defines no signature/hash scheme for the Notify postback, so `src/app/api/webhooks/netcash/route.ts` no longer trusts the posted body — it takes only `RequestTrace` and re-verifies server-to-server via a new `checkPayNowTransactionStatus()`; only that response can mark a `Payment` `SUCCEEDED`. This is the concrete, Pay Now-specific answer to Asana task `1217761345745722` ("confirm Netcash callback contract... verify provider signature against raw body"): there is no provider signature to verify, so authenticity comes from re-verification instead. Separately, the `ValidateServiceKey` SOAP namespace bug (data fields sent in the wrong DataContract namespace, causing every field to silently deserialize to null) was fixed and confirmed live against Netcash's real endpoint on 4 September.
- **Two further pre-existing bugs found and fixed while proving this live, neither introduced by the correction above:**
  1. `src/proxy.ts`'s session-auth allowlist (`publicApiPrefixes`) never included `/api/webhooks/netcash`, so Netcash's postback was rejected with 401 before it ever reached the handler, regardless of portal configuration. Fixed by adding the prefix, with a new regression test in `tests/auth-security.test.ts`.
  2. `getNetcashConnection()` in `netcash-client.ts` returned `IntegrationConnection.config` without decrypting its `*Encrypted` fields, so any real call to `createPayNowCheckout` failed closed with `NETCASH_PAY_NOW_SERVICE_KEY_MISSING` — for every Netcash product, not only Pay Now. Fixed by decrypting with the existing `decryptIntegrationSecret` helper before use. **This fix is proven working only in a disposable, throwaway test environment and has not yet been committed to `main`** — see open gates below.
- **Testing:** full local suite passed 182/182 after the auth-proxy fix and its new regression test (up from 181, which already included 11 new Pay Now tests from the contract-correction slice).
- **Commit, push and merge:** the contract-correction slice merged via PR #29 (squash); the auth-proxy fix merged via PR #30 (squash — this repository requires squash/rebase merges). Both promoted cleanly to `main`.
- **Deployment:** both merges auto-deployed through the existing CI → VPS-deploy pipeline. PR #30 is live as image `stor24-crm:20114bda`; a direct request to `/api/webhooks/netcash` returned 405 (route reachable) instead of 401 (blocked) after that deploy.
- **Live production verification — a real Netcash sandbox transaction:** with the Pay Now service key's NetConnector Profile URLs configured against the correct production domain (`stor24-site.srv938083.hstgr.cloud` — see the domain note below), one real R10.00 sandbox transaction ran through Netcash's own hosted checkout to a genuine "Transaction successful" confirmation. Direct database inspection independently confirmed `Payment.status: "SUCCEEDED"` with a matching `WebhookInbox` row carrying `TransactionAccepted: "true"` and real Netcash-originated delivery headers — proving the server-to-server re-verification path, not the raw postback, is what moved the state. A first attempt before the auth-proxy fix (payment `cmtn0et5m00000ulsmeua3lbs`) did not complete for exactly that reason; the second (payment `cmtqr5pkr00000up7wbig809i`) is the one that succeeded.
- **Domain note:** `stor24-crm-app-1` (this application) is served at `stor24-site.srv938083.hstgr.cloud`. A separate, unrelated container (`stor4`) happens to be served at the similarly named `stor4.srv938083.hstgr.cloud` on the same VPS/Traefik instance — confirmed via each container's own Traefik router-rule label. Earlier manual browser testing against the wrong domain, not any code change, is why the webhook initially appeared to receive nothing.
- **Open gates before this is anything more than a Pay Now sandbox proof:**
  1. Port the `getNetcashConnection()` decryption fix from the disposable test clone into `main` with its own test coverage — without it, the app's own (still-nonexistent) checkout-creation code path cannot work at all.
  2. Prove failed, cancelled, timed-out and duplicate-callback cases, not only the one success above (Asana task `1217761345745722` and `docs/STOR24_OUTSTANDING_TASKS.md` item 1 both still call for this explicitly).
  3. Every other Netcash product in `netcash-client.ts` (eMandate, DebiCheck, standard debit order, AVS, statement) remains unverified scaffold; this slice does not validate them.
  4. Reconciliation to ledger/statement and named exception ownership are unaffected by this slice and remain open.
  5. Business sign-off remains required before Netcash moves off "Configuration required" / item 1 off BLOCKED, per governance already recorded in `claude/invoicing-statements-scope.md` §0.

This is the canonical programme handover. Historical sections below preserve the evidence available when each slice was delivered; where they still say “pending” or quote an older remote head, this snapshot and later dated evidence supersede them. Do not delete the history merely to make the file shorter.

### Current repository and production boundary

- **CRM/operations:** `blendproperty/stor24-portal`, production branch `main`, reviewed remote head `874a5916f351930952af254209645b8c420597e5` (`docs: close customer and lead ownership task`). This handover was prepared in a clean branch from that exact remote head; the old local `codex/fix-facility-patch-validation` checkout is not a promotion source.

The CMS owns editorial content only; it must not become a second customer, lead, reservation or operational database.
- **BlendSign:** `blendproperty/blendsign`, production branch `main`, reviewed remote head `7a66cae3505fb1d5963d9718d34a5bb0b79c8404` (`docs: close BlendSign failure acceptance`). BlendSign owns envelopes, signatures, sealed documents, certificates and signing-delivery evidence; CRM owns tenancy activation and operational state.
- **Live read-only evidence at 15:26 SAST on 1 September 2026:** CRM `/api/health` returned HTTP 200 with application and database `ok`; public `/api/health` returned HTTP 200 with CMS and CRM dependencies healthy; `/`, `/book` and `/space-guide` returned HTTP 200. `/book` was pinned to Midpoint without a store selector. `/pricing` redirected permanently to `/book`; `/locations`, `/locations/sandton` and `/storage-near-me` redirected permanently to `/storage/midpoint`.

4. **Netcash and billing:** Pay Now specifically now has a verified contract, real sandbox credentials, a server-to-server re-verified callback path and one genuine successful sandbox transaction (see "Netcash Pay Now sandbox proof — 7 September 2026" above) — it is no longer a pure simulator for that one product, but it is still gated by `transactionProcessingEnabled` defaulting to false everywhere, and the credential-decryption fix any real checkout depends on is not yet merged to `main`. ### Takeover rules

- Start every slice by fetching the relevant remote, checking the exact checkout/branch/status and reading that repository's current `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`; do not work from the stale nested worktrees in the Sitelink folder.
- Work one bounded slice at a time. Separate implementation, automated testing, commit/push, merge, deployment/configuration, live verification, business UAT and approval in every status update.
- Do not mutate production customer, provider, financial, access-control or signing state without Brett's explicit approval immediately before the action. Use disposable UAT records and retain evidence before cleanup.
- After validation, update every affected canonical `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`, reconcile Asana when in scope, and confirm the context commit exists on the intended remote production branch before calling the slice complete.
- A ready-to-paste continuation brief is maintained in `CLAUDE_CONTINUATION_PROMPT.md`.

## Move-out workflow hardening — 31 August 2026

- Built on clean branch `codex/move-out-workflow` from `origin/main` commit `383cff4`; the obsolete local `codex/fix-facility-patch-validation` checkout and its unrelated changes were not used.
- Move-out input now requires an explicit replay/idempotency key, operational reason/condition notes and coherent deposit treatment (`NONE`, `REFUND_DUE` or `APPLY_TO_BALANCE`). The staff Accounts workflow captures these fields and reuses the same key for a submitted operation.
- Active biometric access is revoked before tenancy closure. A failure therefore stops closure instead of leaving a closed tenancy with live recorded access.
- Occupancies close atomically. Each vacated unit is recalculated against remaining active occupancy, maintenance and reservation claims, producing `OCCUPIED`, `SERVICE`, `RESERVED` or `AVAILABLE` instead of being released unconditionally.
- Final charges and applied deposit credits have stable ledger references. A refund due creates a high-priority staff task rather than claiming that an unprocessed provider refund occurred. Active insurance enrolment is ended at the move-out date.
- `tenancy.moved_out` audit evidence now records the before state, idempotency key, effective date, final charge, deposit decision, notes, access state and resulting unit state. Same-key retries return the completed result and reuse the WhatsApp idempotency key; conflicting retries fail closed.
- Local validation passed: Prisma client generation, TypeScript, 161/161 tests, production build and lint with zero errors (four pre-existing warnings).
- Commit `9225c85` was fast-forwarded to `main`. CI run `#288` and VPS deployment run `#273` completed successfully for that exact revision. Direct production readback returned service `stor24-crm`, application status `ok` and database status `ok` at `2026-08-31T08:21:20.113Z`.
- Controlled production UAT passed on 31 August 2026 with Brett Dovey's explicit approval. Disposable account `ST24-MTGW6EWH` / tenancy `cmtgw6exs000101qulyti11ts` was moved out of Midpoint Unit 106 effective 31 August with R0 final charge, no deposit action and a controlled-UAT condition note. The operation returned `Move-out completed`; a fresh Accounts readback reduced active accounts from 1 to 0 and removed the closed account from the operational list.
- Downstream live evidence passed: Unit 106 recalculated from occupied to `AVAILABLE` at R1,100; the insurance register showed zero active cover and no current tenancies; the facial-access register had no biometric enrolment for this test tenancy, so no provider revocation was required; and the audit trail recorded `tenancy.moved_out` at 10:37 SAST against tenancy `cmtgw6exs000101qulyti11ts` and actor Brett Dovey. With zero charge and no deposit action, no financial ledger entry or refund task was expected. The move-out functional lifecycle is therefore production-proven for this zero-balance/no-deposit path; non-zero final-charge, applied-deposit, refund-task and active-biometric-revocation variants remain covered by automated tests rather than separate live financial/provider mutations.

> Last reviewed: 27 August 2026. Read this file before planning or changing the repository. Update it whenever a material capability, decision, deployment state, or cross-repository contract changes.

## Product identity and non-negotiable boundary

This is **STOR 24**, not SiteLink. It is a purpose-built CRM, operations, leasing, reservation, reporting and integration platform. Other products supplied research evidence only and must not appear as the product identity.

Official CI is documented in `docs/STOR24_BRAND_CI.md`. Use the approved logo files in `public/brand/`, ink `#071411`, cream `#F5F3EA`, orange `#FF5A0A`, and Satoshi typography.

## Repository role

This repository is the internal STOR 24 CRM and operations portal. Despite the GitHub repository name `stor24-portal`, it is not the public marketing website. It owns staff-facing operational truth and exposes a narrowly sanitised public-booking API to the website.

- Repository: `blendproperty/stor24-portal`
- Primary branch: `main`
- Stack: Next.js 16, React 19, TypeScript, Prisma 7 and PostgreSQL
- Canonical transactional schema: `prisma/schema.prisma`
- Architecture: multi-organisation, multi-facility modular monolith

## Branching policy

Branches exist only as short-lived rollback/review points before merging into `main`. Open a branch, get it reviewed and merged, then delete it immediately.

## Netcash payment provider scaffold — 20 August 2026

Brett supplied links to Netcash's developer docs (eMandate synchronous, DebiCheck, Netcash statement, Pay Now, standard debit orders, AVS) and asked for this to be scaffolded as the payment solution that reconciles and sends to MRI.

**What was built, all on `main`:**

- `src/lib/payments/netcash-client.ts` — low-level REST client covering all six Netcash products Brett linked: eMandate (synchronous), DebiCheck (mandate + collection), standard debit orders, Pay Now (hosted checkout), AVS (bank account verification), and statement retrieval. Reads credentials from an `IntegrationConnection` row (`category: "PAYMENTS"`, `provider: "NETCASH"`) rather than raw env vars — matches how every other external provider in this repo is configured, so no schema change was needed.
- `src/lib/payments/netcash-service.ts` — orchestration layer billing-service.ts and staff UI should call: `verifyCustomerBankAccount`, `setUpRecurringCollection` (DebiCheck mandate), `collectMonthlyRent` (submits a collection against an existing mandate, idempotent per account+date), `createOnceOffCheckout` (Pay Now, for deposits/arrears/ad-hoc charges), `submitFallbackDebitOrder` (standard debit order for accounts not on DebiCheck), `getNetcashStatementForReconciliation`. Every call writes/updates a `Payment` row (`provider: "NETCASH"`) and tracks `IntegrationConnection` health (`lastSuccessAt`/`lastFailureAt`/`consecutiveFailures`) the same way other providers are tracked here.
- `src/app/api/webhooks/netcash/route.ts` — inbound notify/callback endpoint. Persists every inbound call verbatim to `WebhookInbox` first (so nothing is lost even if processing throws), matches by `providerRef` to a `Payment`, and on success creates the corresponding `LedgerEntry` (type `PAYMENT`) and enqueues an MRI export. On failure, marks the `Payment` `FAILED`.
- `src/lib/finance/mri-export.ts` — **generic scaffold only, not a real MRI integration.** The MRI decision pack (API vs SFTP file drop vs manual CSV import, exact field mapping, chart-of-accounts mapping) is still open — see Priority next work. This module reuses `WebhookOutbox` as a durable, retryable "ready to export to finance" queue (`destination: "mri://pending-integration-decision"`, a placeholder) so that once the actual MRI integration method is decided, a worker can be pointed at this queue without touching billing-service.ts or the Netcash integration again.

**Deliberately reused existing schema — no migration required.** `Payment`, `LedgerEntry`, `IntegrationConnection`, `WebhookInbox` and `WebhookOutbox` already existed and already cover everything this integration needs (provider/providerRef tracking, idempotency keys, health tracking, durable retryable queues). Nothing new was added to `prisma/schema.prisma`.

**NOT YET LIVE — explicit gaps, in priority order:**

1. **No real Netcash credentials exist anywhere.** No `IntegrationConnection` row has been created for `provider: "NETCASH"` in any environment; every call will throw `NETCASH_NOT_CONFIGURED` until one is created (with real service keys) via whatever admin flow ends up managing `IntegrationConnection` rows.
2. **Endpoint paths and field names in `netcash-client.ts` are best-effort, not confirmed.** The six Netcash docs pages Brett linked were too large to fully read in this environment (each exceeded the fetch tool's output limit); only partial content was captured (e.g. confirmed `ServiceKey` and `BankAccountNumber#` field names for one product). The rest of the field names (`Reference`, `AccountHolderName`, `CollectionAmount`, etc.) and every endpoint path are inferred from Netcash's general API conventions, not verified against the actual docs. **Every endpoint path and payload shape must be checked against Netcash's live documentation (or a sandbox account) before this is used for a real transaction.**
3. **Webhook signature verification is a stub.** `src/app/api/webhooks/netcash/route.ts` has a `// TODO: verify authenticity` comment where Netcash's actual callback signing/hash scheme needs to go. Right now anyone who knows the webhook URL could POST a fake "payment succeeded" event. This must be fixed before going live — it is the single most important gap for security, not just correctness.
4. **No sandbox testing has been done at all.** None of the six functions in `netcash-service.ts` have been exercised against Netcash's sandbox or production, by this assistant or (as far as recorded here) by Brett.
5. **MRI export is a queue with nowhere to go yet** — by design, per the still-open MRI decision pack. `mri-export.ts` needs a real destination (API client, SFTP writer, or scheduled CSV export) once that decision is made.

## Sign-in security hardening — 19 August 2026

Brett asked for a security audit of sign-in/auth across all three repositories ("harden and secure the website for sign-in and prevent hacking"). Full audit findings and fixes below; this repository (the CRM, highest-privilege sign-in surface) was already the most solidly built of the three.

**What was already solid here, confirmed by direct code review (not assumed):**
- Custom JWT auth via `jose`, not a third-party auth library; bcrypt cost-12 password hashing.
- Double-layer authorization: `src/proxy.ts` middleware verifies the session on every request *and* independently re-checks `user.active`/`sessionVersion` against the database — a stolen or stale cookie doesn't survive a password change or deactivation.
- `src/lib/auth-guards.ts` (`requireSession`, `requirePermission`) does a second, independent DB-backed check per route with facility-scoped RBAC, not just a client-side gate.
- Login route: DB-backed rate limiting (5 attempts/15 min), constant-time comparison to resist user-enumeration timing attacks, all attempts audit-logged.
- Cookies: `httpOnly`, `secure` in production, `sameSite: lax`, 8-hour expiry — no token ever stored in localStorage.
- Password policy: 12+ characters, requires mixed case, digit and special character (`src/lib/validators.ts`).
- Server-to-server endpoints authenticate via SHA-256-hashed shared secrets checked with `timingSafeEqual` (separate path from the CSRF/Origin check below, unaffected by that change).

**Fixed this session:**
- **CSRF gap in `src/lib/request-security.ts`.** `sameOrigin()` previously returned `true` when the `Origin` header was simply absent (`!origin || allowed.has(origin)`) — a real bypass, since a crafted cross-site request that omits `Origin` would sail through. Browsers always send `Origin` on same-site mutating fetch/XHR/form requests, so the fix now requires `Origin` to be present and allow-listed for any non-safe HTTP method (`POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`), while leaving safe methods (`GET`/`HEAD`/`OPTIONS`) unaffected.
- **No security headers at the app layer.** Added `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, a restrictive `Permissions-Policy`, HSTS (`max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains; preload`) and a same-origin-only Content-Security-Policy in `next.config.ts` via `headers()`. This is the staff sign-in surface, so it got the strictest CSP of the three repositories (no external script/style/connect sources at all).

**Follow-up completed and live-verified 24 August 2026:**
- **Task 10 staff MFA/2FA is deployed and proven for the organisation owner.** Each authenticated staff user can enroll a standard authenticator app from Settings using a manually entered TOTP setup key. Enrollment does not become active until a valid six-digit code is verified. Authenticator secrets are AES-256-GCM encrypted at rest using a purpose-derived server key; eight recovery codes are shown once, stored only as keyed hashes, and consumed once. An enabled user receives no normal session after the password step: the server issues a five-minute HttpOnly MFA challenge and creates the existing eight-hour session only after a valid authenticator or recovery code. Login failures, enrollment, enablement, recovery-code use/regeneration and disablement are audit-recorded. Disabling requires both the current password and a valid second factor and invalidates existing sessions. CI and VPS deployment succeeded at commit `4a97bf4`. Brett Dovey enrolled Microsoft Authenticator, completed a password-plus-authenticator login and then a recovery-code login; the recovery counter changed from eight to seven and the live audit trail recorded `user.mfa.enabled`, `user.login.mfa_required`, `user.login.recovery_code_used` and `user.login.succeeded`. Rollout to other administrators and facility managers remains an account-by-account operational action. The separate `stor24-cms` admin surface remains outside this repository and still needs its own MFA implementation.
- Password-reset token expiry enforcement was reviewed but not exhaustively traced end-to-end during the audit; creation/hashing (SHA-256, random 32 bytes) looked correct.
- CVE/dependency cross-check against `bcryptjs`, `jose`, `prisma`, `next` versions was not performed — versions are current-generation but not individually checked against known advisories.

## Implemented foundations

- **Reservation WhatsApp date mapping corrected 26 August 2026, pending CI/deployment.** The approved `stor24_order_confirmation_v2` template labels variable `{{4}}` as the start date. Reservation notifications now supply the customer's intended move-in date in a readable South African format, or `To be confirmed` when no date was supplied. Email and SMS continue to communicate the separate hold-expiry deadline. Automatic WhatsApp messaging remains disabled pending controlled production UAT.
- **Owner-controlled WhatsApp automation added 26 August 2026, pending CI/deployment.** The Integrations page now exposes an Organisation Owner-only, audited customer-automation switch backed by the organisation's Twilio WhatsApp `IntegrationConnection`. Automatic sends require explicit customer consent, configured sender/template values, the server safety gate and the database switch; provider secrets remain server-side. The server gate remains closed pending separate approval, and the new database switch defaults off until the owner deliberately enables it for UAT.
- **WhatsApp reservation UAT exposed local-number delivery failure 26 August 2026.** Public reference `ST24-20260826-028A7B` successfully held Unit 57, but Twilio rejected the unchanged local-format recipient `0817088120` with error `21211`. The Twilio server boundary now normalizes valid South African local and `27...` numbers to E.164 `+27...` for SMS and WhatsApp, while rejecting malformed recipients before a provider call. Deployment and retry remain pending.

- **Facility access and office hours code-complete 26 August 2026, pending CI/deployment.** Company → Store information now stores separate facility-specific access and office schedules for weekdays, Saturdays, Sundays and public holidays. Each period can be marked closed or assigned start/end times. Existing single business-hour values are carried forward as office-hour defaults when first edited, and the customer-safe facility API exposes both schedules for public-site presentation without exposing unrelated configuration.

- **WhatsApp customer workflow code-complete 25 August 2026, deployment/UAT pending.** Distinct WhatsApp consent replaces the former SMS-consent reuse. Approved Content Template sends are idempotent and disabled globally by default. Reservation confirmation, payment receipt, access-ready and move-out lifecycle hooks are connected; other approved lifecycle templates are available through a permission-scoped staff endpoint. Signed Twilio callbacks persist queued/sent/delivered/read/failed state, inbound replies create customer tasks, STOP-style replies revoke consent, delivery failures create tasks, and Communications exposes a facility-scoped delivery log plus controlled retry. Automatic sends must remain disabled until all template SIDs, the sender inbound URL, signature validation, delivery/read callbacks, opt-out and failure handling pass production UAT.

- **Redundancy Stage 2 / installable PWA safe shell — code-complete 25 August 2026, not deployed.** The internal operations portal now has a Next.js web-app manifest, approved STOR 24 icons, production-only service-worker registration, a static branded offline shell and a live connectivity indicator in the facility sidebar plus a prominent offline warning. The service worker intercepts navigation only to provide `offline.html` after a network failure. Its persistent cache allowlist contains only that shell, the manifest and approved `/brand/` and `/icons/` assets. It never intercepts or caches `/api/...`, authenticated application pages, login/session responses, cookies, customer/account data, documents, payments, biometrics or credentials, and it provides no background-sync/offline-write queue. Each production build generates a unique service-worker cache version; activation deletes older `stor24-shell-*` caches and claims clients. Manual retry and the browser's `online` event both navigate to `/` rather than reloading `offline.html`, including when the shell was opened directly. Evidence: Next route types and TypeScript passed; lint had only the three pre-existing Twilio/Netcash warnings; all 60 initial tests passed including public-shell and cache-boundary tests; and the full Next.js 16 production build generated `/manifest.webmanifest`. A local standalone-server check returned 200 for the manifest, generated service worker, offline shell and icon; the service-worker response had `no-cache/no-store` and `Service-Worker-Allowed: /`; `/api/v1/accounts` still returned 401 without a session and `/` still redirected to login. The direct-shell retry defect found during manual review was corrected with a dedicated regression test. Supported-browser installation, actual offline launch/update behaviour and production regression checks remain required after an authorised deployment before this task can be described as live-verified.
- **Redundancy Stage 3 read-only offline pilot — deployed and user-verified 25 August 2026.** The dependency-free static `/offline-workspace.html` supports online facility selection, a 12-hour no-PII operational snapshot, passphrase-derived PBKDF2/AES-GCM encryption and IndexedDB storage. The authenticated `/api/v1/offline/snapshot` endpoint is organisation/facility scoped through `operations.view`, explicitly selects unit availability, lead workflow state, reservation state and task state without customer names, contact details, notes, payments, documents or biometrics, sends `no-store`, and audits downloads. The service worker caches only static workspace code; it never intercepts `/api/...` or stores operational payloads in Cache Storage. PR #13 merged as `1cb4713`; post-merge CI #202 and VPS deployment #186 passed. Brett subsequently confirmed online preparation, installed-PWA cold offline unlock and reconnection tests worked.
- **Redundancy Stage 4 offline readiness controls — deployed 25 August 2026, operational UAT pending.** Adds explicit online/offline state, automatic content-revision checks after unlock/reconnection, a passphrase-gated manual refresh, two-hour expiry warning and hard-expiry state. Each device receives a random non-secret identifier and user-entered operational label; snapshot downloads record that identifier/label, content revision and expiry in the existing audit trail. `/offline-readiness` uses `operations.manage`, organisation/facility scope and the audit trail to show the latest preparation per device without receiving passphrases, encryption keys, ciphertext or offline snapshot content. Existing version-1 encrypted copies are upgraded in place after a successful unlock. PR #14 merged as `d695658`; main CI #204 and VPS deployment #188 passed.
- **Redundancy Stage 5 encrypted offline lead outbox — deployed and user-verified 25 August 2026.** The offline workspace captures a narrowly allowlisted new-lead payload after unlock inside the existing encrypted device record, then synchronises idempotently into organisation- and facility-scoped Customer and Lead records when connectivity returns. Brett verified offline capture, reconnection and live lead creation after PR #15, main CI #206 and VPS deployment #190 passed.
- **Redundancy Stage 6 encrypted offline reservation requests — code-complete 27 August 2026, not deployed.** A new lead may optionally carry a specific-unit hold request in the encrypted outbox. Lead sync completes first and supplies non-PII customer/lead identifiers; the separately permission-checked reservation endpoint then atomically changes the chosen unit from `AVAILABLE` to `RESERVED`, creates a 24-hour `OFFLINE_PWA` reservation using the existing unique idempotency key, advances the lead and audits the device request. A stale unit returns an explicit conflict and remains encrypted/editable until staff refresh and choose another unit. ## Ownership decision — APPROVED 17 August 2026, reaffirmed 18 August 2026

**Approved by:** Brett Dovey, Blend Property Group.

```text
STOR 24 CRM (this repository) — operational system of record
  operational customers, leads, deals, facilities, units, reservations,
  leases, workflows, communications, access intent and operational audit
  — this is the ONLY place customer/lead/deal data should live

STOR 24 public portal — customer presentation, captures leads but stores
  them in the CRM

STOR 24 CMS — editorial only, nothing CRM-shaped

MRI Property Central — approved finance system of record
```

**Resolved 18 August 2026:** the CMS's live CRM-shaped collections (`contacts`, `deals`, `activities`, `units`) and their five dashboards were removed from `stor24-cms` — see that repository's `PROJECT_CONTEXT.md`. Brett liked the visual style of those now-removed dashboards; the new `/graphs` executive dashboard in this repository (see Implemented foundations) is the corrected, properly-owned version of that reporting experience.

### Customer and lead ownership task close-out — 31 August 2026

- **Implementation:** no runtime code was required for this governance slice. The approved contract is now reconciled across the canonical CRM, public-portal and CMS contexts: this CRM alone owns persisted customer, lead, reservation, tenancy, occupancy and operational-audit truth; the public portal captures and presents customer journeys through sanitised server-side CRM APIs; the CMS owns editorial content, approved media and publication state only.
- **Testing / validation:** current source was inspected, not inferred. Prisma defines `Customer`, `Lead`, `Reservation`, `Tenancy` and `Occupancy`; `src/lib/public-lead-service.ts` and `src/lib/public-booking-service.ts` persist the public lead and reservation flows; the public API routes delegate to those services. The existing live quote UAT from 24 August remains the production proof that a public quote becomes a CRM `Customer` + `Lead`.
- **Commit and push:** this documentation-only close-out is committed and pushed with the matching context updates in all three repositories. Remote presence must be checked with `git show origin/main:PROJECT_CONTEXT.md` before handoff.
- **Merge:** the context update is promoted directly to `main`; no separate application-code merge is involved.
- **Deployment / configuration:** none required; this slice changes governance documentation only.
- **Live production verification:** no new production mutation was performed. Existing live evidence remains the 24 August quote-to-CRM UAT and the production-confirmed removal of the CMS shadow-CRM collections. This does not close unrelated finance, provider, data, training or approval gates.
- **Follow-up preserved:** the public portal's legacy `/pricing` source still reads the retired CMS `storage-units` endpoint. It must be removed or changed to a sanitised CRM pricing contract before pricing ownership can be called clean; it does not create or store customer/lead records and therefore does not block this customer-and-lead ownership close-out.

The verified 24-hour hold is now implemented behind a disabled rollout gate, including automatic release; viewing appointments and either payment choice are still not implemented.

## Working rules for any AI assistant (selected, most relevant)

1. Inspect branch, status, recent commits, schema and route/service code before making claims or changes.
2. Never deploy, migrate production data, or create real customer records without explicit authority.
3. This environment cannot run `npm run build`/`npm run check` or query the production database directly — say so explicitly rather than implying build/live confidence from code review alone. Verify via the `Deploy to VPS` Actions log.
4. Don't add a new npm dependency to a repo whose Docker build uses `npm ci` unless the lockfile can actually be regenerated correctly — prefer dependency-free solutions (e.g. hand-rolled SVG/CSS charts) when it can't.
5. When a fix touches customer/lead/deal data, check which system is supposed to own it (see Ownership decision) before picking where to write it.
6. When making a large text-file edit via a tool requiring full replacement content, double-check the content variable actually contains the full intended file before submitting — not a placeholder.
7. Update this file after every material change, with dated evidence, not optimistic status language.
8. This environment has no tool to change GitHub repository Settings (e.g. enabling Actions, branch protection) — those changes require the user to make them directly in GitHub's web UI. Don't imply this can be automated from here.
9. **Security fixes need the same evidence discipline as feature work.** A CSRF/header fix pushed to `main` is not "secured" until it's deployed and, ideally, spot-checked live — don't let the language in this file imply otherwise.
10. **When scaffolding a third-party financial integration from docs pages too large to fully read in this environment, say exactly which parts were verified vs inferred — don't let generated code read as more confirmed than it is.** The Netcash scaffold (20 August 2026) is the reference example: field names for five of six products are unverified and this is stated explicitly in the code comments and in this file, not glossed over.

## Definition of done

A CRM capability is complete only when it is database-backed, scoped, permission-enforced, audited, tested, operationally owned and — where an external provider or deployment is involved — configured and proven end to end with reconciliation and exception handling.

**Note on this revision:** condensed from a much longer version to reliably get through after repeated tool timeouts — full historical detail (e-signature v1/v2/v3 narrative, reservation-cancel proof steps, deployment gotchas, numbered working rules 1–23) remains in git history at commit `1f1b4393`/`09cc0a8e`/`04fead0e`/`377eb088` and should be restored/merged forward on the next substantial edit rather than left condensed indefinitely.
### Office-hours-aware viewing protection (2026-08-26)

- Viewing appointments are valid only inside the selected facility's configured office hours.
- The booking horizon is three days so a Friday/Saturday enquiry can select the next Monday opening.
- A verified viewing reservation is protected for at least 24 hours and, when closures push the appointment later, through the appointment plus one hour.
- The hold deadline is inventory protection, not an entitlement to arrive outside the booked appointment or after office closing.

Implement a resend cooldown or stable reminder idempotency key and repeat the mailbox/audit verification before closing this slice.
- Corrective code added 31 August 2026: the resend API now enforces a five-minute server-side cooldown from the persisted document resend audit, returns HTTP 429 plus `Retry-After` for a repeat, and also derives a stable document/time-window BlendSign idempotency key to close the concurrent-request race. The focused reconciliation suite passes 11/11 tests. The wider local command passed 131 tests but six unrelated modules could not load the absent generated Prisma client in this clean worktree; CI/build evidence is still required before this fix is called deployed.
- First production retry of that correction exposed a key-contract defect: BlendSign rejected the colon-delimited deterministic key with `Provide a valid Idempotency-Key header.` No resend audit was written and this is not passing UAT. The deterministic key is now a SHA-256-derived uppercase `REM-` token matching the provider's accepted key shape; deployment and fresh live retry evidence remain required.
- Corrected production retest passed after commit `6feee57` deployed. One new reminder arrived at `doveybrett@gmail.com` at 08:49 SAST. The immediate repeat was blocked with `A signing reminder was sent recently. Please wait before sending another.`, no second new email appeared, and the same document `cmtguh1t200015iu2lgmn6nrw` remained awaiting signature. This closes the duplicate-reminder suppression slice with live UI and mailbox evidence.
- Task 2 invalid-signature production test passed 31 August 2026: a deliberately invalid `envelope.completed` signature returned HTTP 401 with `Invalid webhook signature.` The live integration view remained at 134 processed, 0 pending, 0 failed/dead-letter; the awaiting-signature count, envelope identity and three pre-existing reconciliation records were unchanged. Rejected unauthenticated payloads are intentionally not persisted as trusted webhook inbox events.
- BlendSign contract inspection confirmed unknown merge keys and missing/incorrect role sets are rejected before envelope creation. Executable contract coverage locks those ordering guarantees. Provider-outage inspection found signing-link failures were audited but the queue lacked retry/backoff; BlendSign now schedules signing-link jobs with five exponential-backoff attempts. Final close-out passed 31 August 2026: a dependency-injected non-production SMTP outage forced five failures, audited all five as `delivery_failed`, propagated each retryable error and recorded no false success. BlendSign commit `04afc58d9819d1e1cbe6239748204e1339ad8d1b` is on `main`; VPS deployment `#36` / run `33390291116` succeeded for that exact revision, rebuilding the app and worker. Live `/login` returned HTTP 200 and the unauthenticated health boundary remained HTTP 401. No provider configuration or production recipient changed. Combined with the passed invalid-HMAC production test, resend cooldown/mailbox proof and pre-create key/role validation, the BlendSign failure, security and idempotency acceptance slice is complete.

## Transfer lifecycle hardening — 31 August 2026

- Live transfer UAT cannot begin yet because the production Accounts workspace currently has zero accounts and therefore no legitimate active tenancy to transfer. No transfer mutation was attempted.
- Pre-UAT review found the destination unit was read as `AVAILABLE` and later updated separately, allowing concurrent transfers to select the same destination. The transfer transaction now atomically claims the destination with a conditional `AVAILABLE -> OCCUPIED` `updateMany` and fails with `CONFLICT` unless exactly one row changes.
- The `tenancy.transferred` audit now records the previous and new unit/occupancy identities plus effective date and new monthly rate. Focused automated coverage locks the atomic claim and audit evidence. Deployment plus a disposable signed tenancy are required before controlled live `occupied source -> transfer -> source available / destination occupied` proof.

Approved disposable account `ST24-MTGW6EWH` used BlendSign envelope `cmtgw6f5e002b5iu2nyplrley`; completion advanced the webhook inbox from 134 to 135 processed with 0 pending/failed and activated Unit 105 at R1,200. Functional lifecycle proof is complete. Independently confirm deployment of atomic-claim commit `f0588f2` before closing the concurrency guard itself.
